# Proposed OEIS comments: A218066 to A223240

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A218066

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A218066>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [553](../papers/0501-1000/0553-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) +11*a(n-2) -7*a(n-3) -34*a(n-4) +11*a(n-5) +49*a(n-6) -14*a(n-7) -36*a(n-8) +7*a(n-9) +a(n-10) +8*a(n-11) +2*a(n-12) -13*a(n-13) +2*a(n-14) +4*a(n-15) for n>18

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 65 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 18. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A218185

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A218185>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [9201](../papers/9001-9500/9201-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: n*(n-1)*(n-2)*a(n) -12*(2*n-5)*(n-1)*(n-2)*a(n-1) +4 *(n-2)*(52*n^2-312*n+465) *a(n-2) -48*(2*n-7)*(8*n^2-56*n+95)*a(n-3) +256*(n-5) *(2*n-7) *(2*n-9)*a(n-4)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Oct 08 2016

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 4, so the recurrence holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A218348

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A218348>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [2833](../papers/2501-3000/2833-PROOF.pdf)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 21 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A219314

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A219314>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [9451](../papers/9001-9500/9451-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: n*a(n) -2*n*a(n-1) +11*(-n+2)*a(n-2) +4*(2*n-5)*a(n-3) +8*(5*n-17)*a(n-4) +24*(n-4)*a(n-5)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jun 14 2016
> Conjecture: n*(5*n-7)*a(n) -4*(5*n^2-12*n+6)*a(n-1) -(15*n^2-11*n-30) *a(n-2) +2*(35*n^2-119*n+66)*a(n-3) +12*(n-3)*(5*n2)*a(n-4)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jun 14 2016

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 3, so the recurrence holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A220563

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A220563>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3123](../papers/3001-3500/3123-PROOF.pdf)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 5 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Result obtained: **3 September 2026**  
Paper: [567](../papers/0501-1000/0567-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 36*a(n-2) -200*a(n-4) -40*a(n-6) -84*a(n-8) +80*a(n-10) -20*a(n-12) +80*a(n-14) for n>20

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 481 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 20. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A222099

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A222099>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [8439](../papers/8001-8500/8439-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 42*a(n-1) - 302*a(n-2) + 521*a(n-3) + 114*a(n-4) - 144*a(n-5).
> Empirical g.f.: 2*x*(8 - 71*x + 133*x^2 + 26*x^3 - 36*x^4) / (1 - 42*x + 302*x^2 - 521*x^3 - 114*x^4 + 144*x^5). - _Colin Barker_, Aug 15 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition is carried by a bounded amount of state down the array -- a window of three consecutive lines where it names only cells one step apart, and otherwise a row sum together with one flag per adjacent column pair -- so counting the arrays a line at a time gives a transfer matrix with 7 states, and a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 5. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A222382>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [892](../papers/0501-1000/0892-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 8*a(n-1) + 8*a(n-2) - 64*a(n-3) - 16*a(n-4) + 128*a(n-5).
> Empirical g.f.: 8*x*(1 - 2*x + 8*x^2 + 16*x^4) / ((1 - 2*x)^2*(1 + 2*x)^2*(1 - 8*x)). - _Colin Barker_, Aug 16 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 55 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 5. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A222439

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A222439>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [9021](../papers/9001-9500/9021-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 18*a(n-1) - 27*a(n-2).
> Empirical g.f.: 3*x*(3 - 5*x) / (1 - 18*x + 27*x^2). - _Colin Barker_, Mar 15 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition is carried by a bounded amount of state down the array -- a window of three consecutive lines where it names only cells one step apart, and otherwise a row sum together with one flag per adjacent column pair -- so counting the arrays a line at a time gives a transfer matrix with 3 states, and a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 2. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A223056

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A223056>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [3124](../papers/3001-3500/3124-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 18*a(n-1) - 71*a(n-2) + 102*a(n-3) - 48*a(n-4) for n>5.
> Empirical g.f.: x*(2 - 26*x + 76*x^2 - 73*x^3 + 16*x^4) / ((1 - x)*(1 - 2*x)*(1 - 15*x + 24*x^2)). - _Colin Barker_, Aug 16 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition is stated about connected sets of equal values, but a connected set fails to lie in a straight line exactly when it contains a turn -- a cell with an equal neighbour beside it and an equal neighbour above or below it -- since a connected set with both a horizontal and a vertical join has two consecutive joins of different kinds, and they share such a cell. Whether a cell has an equal neighbour beside it is decided by its own row, so the whole condition is a condition on pairs of adjacent rows, and counting a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 5 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 5. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition is stated about connected sets of equal values, but a connected set fails to lie in a straight line exactly when it contains a turn -- a cell with an equal neighbour beside it and an equal neighbour above or below it -- since a connected set with both a horizontal and a vertical join has two consecutive joins of different kinds, and they share such a cell. Whether a cell has an equal neighbour beside it is decided by its own row, so the whole condition is a condition on pairs of adjacent rows, and counting a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 18 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 12. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A223211

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A223211>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3389](../papers/3001-3500/3389-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) + 4*a(n-2) = 6*A063782(n-1).
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Aug 17 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 6 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Aug 17 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```

```
